Integrated Assessment of Safety The Applicant’s Position: Pembrolizumab, as neoadjuvant therapy and continued as adjuvant therapy in combination with RT, with or without cisplatin followed by pembrolizumab monotherapy, has a manageable safety profile that is generally consistent with the established safety profile of pembrolizumab monotherapy, and the individual safety profiles of RT and cisplatin. No new safety concerns for pembrolizumab were identified. AEOSI in the pembrolizumab group were generally consistent with the established safety profile of pembrolizumab monotherapy in type and severity, and no new indication-specific, immune- mediated AEs causally associated with pembrolizumab were identified when pembrolizumab was added to RT ± cisplatin. The FDA’s Assessment: In general, FDA agrees with the Applicant’s position. The addition of single-agent pembrolizumab to SoC therapy compared to SoC therapy alone in the ITT population did not appear to compromise surgical resectability  SRVWဨVXUJLFDO PRUELGLW\ DQG PRUWDOLW\ , or ability to receive adjuvant radiation therapy. However, in an exploratory subgroup analysis of patients with PD-L1 positive hypopharyngeal primary tumors (n=51), patients on the pembrolizumab arm had lower rates of complete (R0) surgical resection compared to patients on the SoC arm (74% vs 87%). Overall safety outcomes in terms of TEAEs that led to death, SAEs, and Grade 3 to 4 TEAEs were associated with slightly higher rates in the pembrolizumab arm compared to the SoC arm. However, no new safety signals were identified and the safety profile was generally consistent with the known toxicities of the components of the study therapy. No updates were made to the current Warnings and Precautions section of the USPI for pembrolizumab (Section 5). The incidence and severity of immune-related adverse events (irAEs) in the pembrolizumab arm were also consistent with the known toxicity profile of pembrolizumab as described in the approved product labeling for pembrolizumab. Due to the lack of data to demonstrate the contribution of the radiation and adjuvant post-radiation phases of treatment to the overall safety Reference ID: 5607873

<<<PAGE 311>>>
NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation {Insert Application Type and Number} {Insert Product Trade and Generic Name} 121 Version date: March 1, 2024 (ALL NDA/ BLA reviews) Disclaimer: In this document, the sections labeled as “Data” and “The Applicant’s Position” are completed by the Applicant and do not necessarily reflect the positions of the FDA. Confidential and efficacy of the treatment regimen, FDA specifically analyzed the risk profile, including irAEs, of the adjuvant phase of treatment with pembrolizumab. Although the observed safety profile was generally consistent with the known safety profile of pembrolizumab monotherapy, unresolved and long-term sequelae of irAEs were observed. A significant review issue for this application was an inability to assess the contribution of all phases of this perioperative regimen, with the most concern focused on the uncertainty around the need for pembrolizumab in combination with radiation (with or without cisplatin) followed by an additional 12 cycles as a single agent in the adjuvant phase of therapy. The safety analysis was particularly important for this application given that the risk of overtreatment is proportional to the severity of the toxicities observed in the adjuvant phase. However, adverse event analyses and PRO data supported that single agent pembrolizumab was relatively well tolerated in the adjuvant phase. The review team assessed that the potential benefits of the perioperative pembrolizumab-based regimen outweighed the uncertainty around the contribution of phase of treatment. Importantly, studies of future perioperative regimens for HNSCC including pembrolizumab or other add-on drugs should be designed to improve the ability to demonstrate the contribution of pembrolizumab in the neoadjuvant and adjuvant phases of therapy. SUMMARY AND CONCLUSIONS 8.3. Statistical Issues The FDA’s Assessment: The Applicant submitted data and results from EFS, OS, and mPR across 3 populations: PD-L1 CPS  10, PD-L1 CPS  1, and all patients, for the comparison of perioperative pembrolizumab in combination with SoC therapy versus SoC in patients with Stage III-IVA resectable LA HNSCC. According to the multiple testing hierarchy, EFS was to be tested in patients with tumor PD-L1 CPS  ILUVW IROORZHG E\ PD-L1 &36  DQG ILQDOO\ in the ITT population. At IA1, EFS crossed the boundary for statistical significance in the PD-L1 CPS  10 [HR = 0.66, 95% CI (0.49, 0.88), p=0.00217 @ , PD-L1 CPS  1 [HR = 0.70 95% CI (0.55, 0.89), p=0.0124 @ , and ITT populations [HR = 0.73, 95% CI (0.58, 0.92), p=0.00411 @ . At the time of the IA1 cutoff, OS did not cross the boundary in the PD-L1 &36  SRSXODWLRQ DQG VWDWLVWLFDO WHVWLQJ ZDV VWRSSHG mPR was statistically significant with an mPR rate difference of 13.7% (95% CI: 9.7, 18.7, p < 0.00001) in the PD-L1 CPS  10 population, 9.8% (95% CI: 7.0, 13.3, p < 0.00001) in the PD-L1 CPS  1 population, and 9.3% (95% CI: 6.7, 12.8, p < 0.00001) in the ITT population. Although mPR was analyzed with appropriate Type-1 error control this endpoint has not been validated as an early endpoint likely to predict clinical benefit in resectable HNSCC. It is uncertain whether improvements in mPR translate into improvements in long-term outcomes of EFS and OS in the perioperative treatment setting. There is insufficient evidence to support the Reference ID: 5607873

<<<PAGE 312>>>
NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation {Insert Application Type and Number} {Insert Product Trade and Generic Name} 122 Version date: March 1, 2024 (ALL NDA/ BLA reviews) Disclaimer: In this document, the sections labeled as “Data” and “The Applicant’s Position” are completed by the Applicant and do not necessarily reflect the positions of the FDA. Confidential use of this endpoint in the regulatory setting. Patients in the standard of care arm did not receive treatment in the neoadjuvant period of the trial, and therefore did not have an opportunity to achieve mPR. Accordingly, there were no patients with mPR in the SoC arm. Additionally, mPR reflects the benefit of neoadjuvant treatment only, and does not predict the benefit of the overall perioperative treatment including the adjuvant phase of therapy. As mentioned in Section 8.1.2 of the Assessment Aid, the small sample size of the PD-L1 negative subgroup (n=27) does not properly allow for a definitive assessment of benefit/risk. In this small subgroup, the EFS HR was 2.57 (95% CI: 0.50, 13.36) and the OS HR was of 1.73 (95% CI: 0.32, 9.54), suggesting a potential lack of benefit in patients treated with pembrolizumab. Because of the small number of patients and events in the two arms and the wide confidence interval, this result should be interpreted with caution in the context of the known mechanism of action of pembrolizumab and other available information as discussed in Section 8.1.2 Efficacy Results – Primary Endpoint (Including Sensitivity Analyses) of the Assessment Aid. Another important subgroup finding in a small subgroup was in patients with primary hypopharyngeal tumors. For patients with PD-L1 positive (CPS  1) hypopharyngeal tumors, the EFS hazard ratio was 2.28 (95% CI: 0.79, 6.56) and the OS hazard ratio was 2.03 (0.62, 6.60), suggesting a detriment in patients treated with pembrolizumab. In contrast, treatment with pembrolizumab was favored for patients with PD-L1 positive HNSCC with all other primary tumor sites: for laryngeal tumors, the EFS HR was 0.62 (95% CI: 0.36, 1.06) and the OS HR was 0.65 (95% CI: 0.38, 1.14); for oral cavity tumors, the EFS HR was 0.71 (95% CI: 0.53, 0.95) and the OS HR was 0.74 (95% CI: 0.54, 1.02); and for oropharyngeal tumors, the EFS HR was 0.32 (95% CI: 0.15, 0.68) and the OS HR was 0.38 (95% CI: 0.15, 0.93) (see Table 16 ). The hypopharyngeal subgroup is notably small, and the wide confidence intervals around the hazard ratio indicate that results should be interpreted with caution; nevertheless, the EFS result for this subgroup raises questions about the benefit of treatment with pembrolizumab for patients with primary hypopharyngeal tumors. Overall, the primary analysis results of KEYNOTE-689 provide statistically persuasive evidence of improvement in EFS for patients with resectable, PD- / SRVLWLYH &36 LA HNSCC when treated with perioperative pembrolizumab. Results from secondary endpoints also support the effectiveness of this perioperative pembrolizumab-based treatment for these patients. 8.4. Conclusions and Recommendations The FDA’s Assessment: The primary trial supporting this sBLA is KEYNOTE-689, a multicenter, global, open-label, randomized trial in patients with resectable Stage III to IVA HNSCC, per the AJCC 8th edition staging system. However, this trend in a potential detriment is consistent with prior findings with pembrolizumab monotherapy for patients with PD-L1 negative recurrent or metastatic HNSCC, both in the first-line treatment setting (i.e., in the KEYNOTE-048 trial) and the refractory treatment setting (i.e., in KEYNOTE-040). The mechanism of action of pembrolizumab also provides scientific rationale that the efficacy of pembrolizumab monotherapy may be limited in patients with PD-L1 negative tumors. Confidential given the safety profile of pembrolizumab in the adjuvant setting. The results from KEYNOTE-689 meet the statutory evidentiary standard for traditional approval and provide substantial evidence of effectiveness of pembrolizumab for adult patients with resectable locally advanced HNSCC whose tumors express PD- / &36  DV GHWHUPLQHG E\ DQ FDA-approved test, as neoadjuvant treatment, continued as adjuvant treatment in combination with RT with or without cisplatin, and then as a single agent. The magnitude of the EFS treatment effect, with a HR of 0.70 (95% CI: 0.55, 0.89; p=0.00140) and corresponding to 30.1- month improvement in median EFS, is statistically significant and clinically meaningful, and represents a major improvement for the treatment of patients with resectable, LA HNSCC. These results are also supported by a trend towards improved OS with pembrolizumab. Overall, data from KEYNOTE-689 support a favorable benefit:risk assessment for perioperative pembrolizumab for patients with resectable, LA HNSCC. Therefore, the FDA review team recommends traditional approval for the following indication: KEYTRUDA for the treatment of adult patients with resectable locally advanced HNSCC whose tumors express PD- / >&RPELQHG 3RVLWLYH 6FRUH &36 @ as determined by an FDA-approved test, as a single agent as neoadjuvant treatment, continued as adjuvant treatment in combination with radiotherapy (RT) with or without cisplatin and then as a single agent. 8.4.1. Confidential 12 Risk Evaluation and Mitigation Strategies (REMS) The FDA’s Assessment: There were no significant safety concerns identified during the review of this supplement requiring risk management beyond labeling or warranting consideration for a Risk Evaluation and Mitigation Strategy to ensure safe use of perioperative pembrolizumab for patients with resectable LA HNSCC as in the proposed indication. 13 Postmarketing Requrements and Commitment The FDA’s Assessment: 7KH IROORZLQJ 3RVWဨ0DUNHWLQJ &RPPLWPHQWV ZHUH LVVXHG DQG DJUHHG XSRQ ZLWK WKH $SSOLFDQW PMC 1 (Clinical) PMC Rationale: Traditional approval was granted for perioperative single agent pembrolizumab as neoadjuvant and adjuvant therapy added to standard of care (SoC) surgery and postoperative radiation or chemoradiation for patients with resectable locally advanced head and neck squamous cell carcinoma (LA HNSCC). Approval was based on a clinically meaningful improvement in event- free survival (EFS) demonstrated in the KEYNOTE-689 trial. The current overall survival (OS) data from the KEYNOTE-689 trial are immature. The results of the final OS analysis will better characterize the benefits and risks of perioperative pembrolizumab for patients with resectable LA HNSCC and may potentially inform updated labeling. PMC Language: Complete clinical trial, KEYNOTE-689 (NCT03765918), titled, “Study of Pembrolizumab Given Prior to Surgery and in Combination With Radiotherapy Given Post-surgery for Advanced Head and Neck Squamous Cell Carcinoma (MK-3475-689)” to provide long- term efficacy data. Provide the final overall survival (OS) analysis once the required number of events for OS, as prespecified in the statistical analysis plan, have occurred in the intention-to-treat population. 1 INTRODUCTION On December 23, 2024, Merck Sharp & Dohme LLC, a subsidiary of Merck & Co., Inc. submitted for the Agency’s review a Prior Approval Supplement (PAS) – Efficacy to their approved Biologics License Application (BLA) 125514/S-180 for KEYTRUDA (pembrolizumab) injection. With this submission, the Applicant proposes KEYTRUDA (pembrolizumab) injection for the treatment of patients with resectable locally advanced head and neck squamous cell cancer as neoadjuvant treatment, continued as adjuvant treatment in combination with radiotherapy with or without platinum-containing chemotherapy, and then as a single agent. This collaborative review is written by the Division of Medical Policy Programs (DMPP) and the Office of Prescription Drug Promotion (OPDP) in response to a request by the Division of Oncology II (DO2) on January 21, 2025, for DMPP and OPDP to review the Applicant’s proposed Medication Guide (MG) for KEYTRUDA (pembrolizumab) injection. 2 MATERIAL REVIEWED x Draft KEYTRUDA (pembrolizumab) injection MG received on December 23, 2024, and received by DMPP and OPDP on May 22, 2025. x Draft KEYTRUDA (pembrolizumab) injection PI received on December 23, 2024, revised throughout the review cycle, and received by DMPP and OPDP on May 22, 2025. x Approved KEYTRUDA (pembrolizumab) injection MG dated January 28, 2025 and PI April 21, 2025. 3 REVIEW METHODS To enhance patient comprehension, materials should be written at a 6 th to 8 th grade reading level, and have a reading ease score of at least 60%. A reading ease score of 60% corresponds to an 8 th grade reading level. Additionally, in 2008 the American Society of Consultant Pharmacists Foundation (ASCP) in collaboration with the American Foundation for the Blind (AFB) published Guidelines for Prescription Labeling and Consumer Medication Information for People with Vision Loss . The ASCP and AFB recommended using fonts such as Verdana, Arial or APHont to make medical information more accessible for patients with vision loss. In our collaborative review of the MG we: x simplified wording and clarified concepts where possible x ensured that the MG is consistent with the PI x removed unnecessary or redundant information x ensured that the MG is free of promotional language or suggested revisions to ensure that it is free of promotional language Reference ID: 5599284

x ensured that the MG meets the Regulations as specified in 21 CFR 208.20 x ensured that the MG meets the criteria as specified in FDA’s Guidance for Useful Written Consumer Medication Information (published July 2006) 4 CONCLUSIONS The MG is acceptable with our recommended changes. 5 RECOMMENDATIONS x Please send these comments to the Applicant and copy DMPP and OPDP on the correspondence. x Our collaborative review of the MG is appended to this memorandum. Clinical Inspection Summary BLA 125514 S180/ Pembrolizumab 2 Of note, the inspection results of Dr. Brana Garcia, are based on a summary provided by the FDA field investigator and are therefore preliminary. If significantly new or different information is contained in the final FDA Establishment Inspection Report, an addendum to this clinical inspection summary will be filed. II. BACKGROUND Merck Sharp & Dohme LLC submitted BLA125514 S-180 seeking approval for pembrolizumab to treat resectable locally advanced HNSCC as neoadjuvant and adjuvant treatment combined with radiotherapy (RT), with or without platinum-containing chemotherapy, and then as a single agent. The primary evidence supporting this indication is based on data from Study MK-3475-689. Pembrolizumab is currently approved for various adult solid tumors and hematologic malignancies, including first-line treatment of advanced HNSCC, alone or combined with platinum and 5FU chemotherapy, for first-line and previously treated, advanced metastatic, unresectable, recurrent HNSCC. MK-3475-689 is a phase III, randomized, multicenter, open-label, active-control trial in subjects with resectable locally advanced (Stage III-IVA) HNSCC. Eligible subjects were randomized 1:1 to either: x Arm A: Neoadjuvant pembrolizumab before surgical resection, followed by pembrolizumab with radiation plus cisplatin or radiation alone, then pembrolizumab alone x Arm B: Surgery followed by cisplatin or radiation alone Randomization was stratified by primary tumor site, tumor stage, and PD-L1 status. Treatment with pembrolizumab continued until disease progression (as defined by RECISTv1.1 and assessed by BICR), completion of treatment (17 cycles), disease progression precluding definitive surgery, disease recurrence in the adjuvant phase, disease progression for those who did not undergo surgery or had incomplete resection, or unacceptable toxicity. The primary efficacy endpoint was event-free survival (EFS) by BICR. Key secondary endpoints were major pathological response (mPR) rate and overall survival (OS). Informed consent was required before any study-related procedure. Screening procedures, including tumor assessment, medical history, physical examination, laboratory tests, and imaging, were performed within 28 days of treatment initiation. Tumor imaging was performed at screening, prior to surgery, and at regular intervals Reference ID: 5597080

<<<PAGE 354>>>
Clinical Inspection Summary BLA 125514 S180/ Pembrolizumab 3 during the adjuvant phase. After Year 5, participants entered Survival Follow-up every 12 weeks until death, withdrawal of consent, or study end. At the data cut-off date (July 25, 2024), the ongoing study had randomized 714 subjects (263 in the pembrolizumab arm, 351 in the control arm) across 192 sites in 24 countries, including 98 subjects at 39 US sites. III. RESULTS (by site) 1. Dr. Irene Brana Garcia (Site # 1200 ) Passeig De La Vall Dhebron 119-129 Barcelona, SPAIN Inspection dates: May 5 – 9, 2025 Dr. Garcia was inspected as a BIMO review-based inspection for Study MK-3475-689. This was the first inspection for this investigator. At the time of the data cut-off date, the site had screened 16 subjects and randomized 15 in Study MK-3475-689, of whom 10 subjects completed study treatment and are in the follow-up phase, 5 subjects had died. Source records for all 15 subjects randomized at the site were reviewed. Records reviewed included, but were not limited to informed consent forms, eligibility criteria, and adverse event reporting. No significant discrepancies were found when source data were compared with the data submitted to the BLA. The inspection also verified that radiology studies to assess the primary efficacy endpoint were performed according to protocol-specified time points and all scans were submitted for central review. Based on the preliminary results provided by the FDA field investigator, data generated by Dr. Garcia appear to be acceptable in support of the BLA. 2. Dr. Anupama Nehra (Site # 2053 ) 205 South Orange Ave A-1115, Newark New Jersey, 07101 Inspection dates: March 17 – 21 and 24, 2025 Dr. Nehra was inspected as a BIMO review-based inspection for Study MK-3475-689. Clinical Inspection Summary BLA 125514 S180/ Pembrolizumab 4 This was the first FDA inspection for this investigator. At the time of the inspection, the site had screened 9 subjects and enrolled 7 subjects. Of these, 2 were in the follow-up phase, 4 subjects had died, and 1 subject was withdrawn due to non-compliance. Source records for all 7 enrolled subjects were reviewed and compared with the data listings submitted to the BLA. Records reviewed included but were not limited to, informed consent forms, eligibility criteria, radiation therapy, adherence to protocol, concomitant medications, protocol deviation logs, investigational product handling accountability records, and adverse event reporting. No significant or impactful discrepancies were found when source data were compared with the data submitted to the BLA. The inspection verified that imaging scans to evaluate the primary efficacy endpoint were performed as specified in the protocol and all scans were submitted for central review, consistent with the data submitted to the BLA. No significant discrepancies regarding the primary endpoint data were observed. Additional records reviewed during the inspection included but were not limited to, staff training information, CR/sponsor monitoring activities documents, electronic records, and IRB approvals and communications. The inspection observed some poor record- keeping practices which appear non-critical to the safety or efficacy of the drug or subject safety during the trial. Dr. Nehra acknowledged the deficiencies in her April 5, 2025, response. She implemented a CAPA, where applicable, and her response is adequate. Despite this, and based on the inspection, the data generated at Dr. Nehra’s site appear acceptable in support of the proposed indication in the BLA. 3. Merck Sharp & Dohme Corp. 2025 E. IND 122325 Page 2 U.S. Food and Drug Administration Silver Spring, MD 20993 www.fda.gov BACKGROUND On September 26, 2024, Merck, Sharp and Dohme LLC (Merck) submitted a meeting request to discuss acceptability of the efficacy and safety results from interim analysis 1 (IA1) of the KEYNOT-689 trial to support evaluation of a supplemental Biological License Application (sBLA) to add pembrolizumab in combination with standard of care (SoC) therapy as a new indication for locally advanced HNSCC to the US Prescribing Information (USPI) for KEYTRUDA. The meeting was granted as a Type B pre-sBLA meeting on October 7, 2024. The meeting package was received on October 24, 2024. Regulatory History On July 17, 2017, a Type B/End-of-Phase meeting was held to discuss the design of KEYNOTE-689. At that time, FDA indicated that the proposed trial design would not be able to isolate the benefit of neoadjuvant pembrolizumab from post-surgical adjuvant pembrolizumab. On June 25, 2018, a Type C meeting was held to discuss central pathological assessment for determination of risk categorization during the adjuvant phase. FDA acknowledged the challenges associated with central review and timely initiation of adjuvant treatment within 6 weeks of surgery, and therefore did not object to randomization based on local pathology assessment of the presence of high-risk features. On October 3, 2024, an informal teleconference was held to discuss the Top-Line results from IA1 of KEYNOTE-689. FDA agreed that Merck could submit a request for a pre-sBLA meeting based on IA1 data. Additionally, FDA indicated specific information should be included in the meeting background package, including subgroup analyses (e.g., based on PD-L1 combined positive score [CPS] <1, CPS 1-9  DQG &36  ), and and rationale to support the contribution of the neoadjuvant and adjuvant phases of the investigational regimen. Clinical Merck proposes to submit a supplemental marketing application for pembrolizumab for the treatment of patients with resectable, locoregionally advanced HNSCC as neoadjuvant treatment, then continued as adjuvant treatment in combination with standard of care radiation therapy (RT) with or without cisplatin chemotherapy, followed by continuation of pembrolizumab as a single agent. Study KEYNOTE-689 KEYNOTE-689 is a randomized, active-controlled, multi-site, open label study of neoadjuvant pembrolizumab followed by surgery and adjuvant pembrolizumab in combination with standard of care (SoC) RT with or without concurrent cisplatin, with subsequent pembrolizumab monotherapy to complete up to 17 cycles; versus SoC Reference ID: 5501943

Proposed content and format for the BLA Merck’s proposal for the content and format of the BLA submission package is as follows: x Module 2.5 will include a clinical overview of efficacy, safety and benefit/risk analysis. x Module 2.7.3 will include the summary of clinical efficacy; however Merck will only provide a cover page as the integrated summary of efficacy since only 1 study (KEYNOTE-689) is intended to support the efficacy for this BLA filing. This module will also include analyses for CPS <1 and CPS 1-9 populations for EFS, OS, and mPR and the Assessment Aid. x Justification for Contribution of Phase for the neoadjuvant and adjuvant portions of the trial will be provided in Modules 2.7.3 and/or 2.5. x Module 2.7.4 will include the summary of clinical safety and the narrative portion of the integrated summary of safety (ISS). x Module 5.2 will include an appendix describing the rationale for the Applicability of Foreign Data to the US Population and US Medical Practice. x Module 5.3.5.3 will include the ISS. In the ISS and the summary of clinical safety (SCS), data will be organized into tables to include 4 columns: the pembrolizumab arm of KEYNOTE-689 (n=361), the SoC arm of KEYNOTE-689 (n=315), pooled safety data from patients with HNSCC treated with pembrolizumab monotherapy (n=909) and pooled reference safety data for pembrolizumab monotherapy (n=2799). IND 122325 Page 8 U.S. Food and Drug Administration Silver Spring, MD 20993 www.fda.gov FDA sent Preliminary Comments to Merck on November 20, 2024. On November 22, 2024, Merck provided responses and requested to further discuss Question 2 and FDA Additional Comment 12. SPONSOR QUESTIONS AND FDA RESPONSES 1. Does the Agency agree that the efficacy and safety data from Interim Analysis 1 (IA1) from KN689 in this patient population are sufficient to support evaluation of an sBLA for pembrolizumab for the treatment of patients with resectable locoregionally advanced HNSCC as neoadjuvant treatment, then continued as adjuvant treatment in combination with radiation with or without cisplatin chemotherapy, followed by pembrolizumab monotherapy. FDA Response: The efficacy and safety data from Interim Analysis 1 of Study KEYNOTE-689 appear acceptable to support the filing of a marketing application. A final determination regarding filing of the sBLA will be made after review of the contents of the submitted application. Whether the efficacy and safety results indicate a favorable benefit/risk profile and support approval of the sBLA in the proposed patient population will be determined during review of the marketing application. Merck’s 11/22/24 email response: The Sponsor acknowledges FDA ̓ s comment. No further discussion is required. Discussion during the 11/25/2024 meeting: No discussion occurred. 2. The Sponsor is planning to submit a dossier consisting of the KEYNOTE-689 CSR as the pivotal study. Additionally, Merck is planning to provide Modules 2.2, 2.5, 2.7.3, 2.7.4, 5.2, an ISS (Module 5.3.5.3), and an Assessment Aid. Since the submission is based on a single pivotal study (KEYNOTE-689), no ISE will be included except for a reference page to that effect. Modules 2.7.1 and 2.7.2 in the existing lifecycle are considered current and will be referenced in the planned sBLA submission. Module 5.2 will be included to support Applicability of Foreign Data in review of the application. Does the Agency agree that a dossier with these components would be acceptable to support evaluation of the sBLA? FDA Response: The proposed components for a dossier are generally acceptable with one exception. An Integrated Summary of Efficacy (ISE) is required in applications submitted to the FDA in accordance with 21 CFR 314.50(d)(5)(v) and an ISE should be included for this sBLA. In the sBLA, please include an ISE that provides topline data from any other clinical trials, including descriptive investigator-initiated trials exploring pembrolizumab as part of Reference ID: 5501943

IND 122325 Page 9 U.S. Food and Drug Administration Silver Spring, MD 20993 www.fda.gov adjuvant and/or neoadjuvant therapy for locally advanced HNSCC, particularly for resectable disease. The ISE may also include data from trials of pembrolizumab for locally advanced HNSCC treated with definitive radiation or chemoradiation. These data may be helpful in our overall benefit:risk assessment as well as our assessment of the inclusion of both the neoadjuvant and adjuvant components of the investigational therapy, particularly as KEYNOTE-689 does not isolate the contribution of effect of these treatment components. We also request these additional data and exploratory descriptive analyses: a. Data on time intervals between all treatment phases. For each patient and averaged for each treatment arm, please provide time intervals for: o Randomization to surgery or neoadjuvant pembrolizumab o Neoadjuvant pembrolizumab to surgery o Surgery to RT with or without cisplatin o RT with or without cisplatin to adjuvant pembrolizumab b. For both treatment arms, provide mPR and EFS results by N stage, T stage, primary tumor site, and HPV status for oropharyngeal tumors. c. For patients receiving chemoradiation therapy, provide the average number of cycles received and average total exposure of cisplatin for each treatment arm. d. For all patients, provide information on these baseline characteristics for each treatment arm: o Status of surgical margins and definition of clear margins o Classification of surgical resection as R0, R1, or R2 e. Please provide PRO-CTCAE results in a stacked bar chart form within or as an appendix to the assessment aid. We may request additional reports and analyses during our review of the sBLA. Please also see FDA Additional Comments 8 to 12. Please provide PRO-CTCAE results in a stacked bar chart form within or as an appendix to the assessment aid. Whether the EFS results based on the censoring rules of the primary analysis or the sensitivity analysis would be more appropriate for the label will be a review issue. Merck’s 11/22/24 email response: The Sponsor acknowledges FDA’s additional comment. No further discussion is required. Discussion during the 11/25/2024 meeting: No discussion occurred. 11. Provide an anticipated timeline for the protocol-specified primary analyses of OS comparing the pembrolizumab group to the SoC group. Reference ID: 5501943

<<<PAGE 376>>>
IND 122325 Page 15 U.S. Food and Drug Administration Silver Spring, MD 20993 www.fda.gov Merck’s 11/22/24 email response: The prespecified timing of Interim Analysis 2 (IA2) for OS will occur when ~187 deaths in the CS Ӌ 10 population have been observed and ~22 months after the last participant randomized (Section 3.7.1 in sSAP). If the events accrue slower than expected, the analysis can be delayed up to 3 months. The Sponsor anticipates the IA2 data cut-off will occur as early as 25-AUG-2025, but no later than 25-NOV-2025. It is expected the topline results will be available between November 2025 and February 2026. No further discussion is required. Discussion during the 11/25/2024 meeting: No discussion occurred. 12. KEYNOTE-689 did not evaluate the contribution of the neoadjuvant and adjuvant phases of therapy with pembrolizumab for patients with resectable HNSCC and there is concern for potential overtreatment, particularly in the adjuvant phase. An assessment of the effect of each phase of therapy is necessary to better characterize the relative contribution of these phases to the overall outcomes with the perioperative regimen. Provide an integrated analysis of completed, ongoing, and planned trials of perioperative pembrolizumab for patients with resectable HNSCC to evaluate the contribution of phase of pembrolizumab when given as neoadjuvant treatment, as adjuvant treatment concurrent with standard of care adjuvant radiation with or without platinum chemotherapy, and as single agent adjuvant treatment after other treatments are completed. This analysis should include data from the KEYNOTE-412 and GORTEC 2015-01 trials and any updated data from the KEYCHAIN trial. This analysis should also include an evaluation of safety, including immune mediated adverse reactions that occur during each phase of treatment. Include also, as proposed in the Type B pre-sBLA Meeting Package, the unstratified subgroup analysis of EFS in patients who have and have not achieved a pathologic response to explore the contribution of neoadjuvant pembrolizumab to treatment efficacy. Provide any additional analyses from KEYNOTE-689 that provide justification for each phase of pembrolizumab therapy. Merck’s 11/22/24 email response: The Sponsor acknowledges FDA ̓ s additional comment. As requested, the Sponsor will provide descriptive summaries consisting of the studies referenced in Question 2 in order to evaluate the contribution of phase of pembrolizumab when given as neoadjuvant treatment, as adjuvant treatment Reference ID: 5501943

<<<PAGE 377>>>
IND 122325 Page 16 U.S. Food and Drug Administration Silver Spring, MD 20993 www.fda.gov concurrent with standard of care adjuvant radiation with or without platinum chemotherapy, and as single agent adjuvant treatment after other treatments are completed. The Sponsor would like to clarify that the only data currently available for the KEYCHAIN trial are the results of the lead-in phase published and presented in 20197. The ADRISK trial is an ongoing randomized open-label phase II comparing adjuvant pembrolizumab plus post-operative chemoradiotherapy with cisplatin versus post-operative chemoradiotherapy with cisplatin for patients with resectable LA HNSCC who have undergone surgery and are found to have intermediate or high-risk pathologic features. This trial evaluates adjuvant only pembrolizumab but cannot be included in this analysis at this time because the trial is ongoing and the primary analysis has not been completed. As stated in the response to Question 2, the Sponsor does not have direct access to the patient-level data for this or other investigator-initiated trials evaluating pembrolizumab. Therefore, this descriptive summary will not include the ADRISK and KEYCHAIN trials. The analysis will include the following: o Trials evaluating pembrolizumab as perioperative treatment for patients with resectable LA HNSCC (NCT02296684, and NCT02641093). o Trials evaluating pembrolizumab for patients with unresected LA HNSCC (i.e. not amenable to primary surgery) requiring definitive CRT or RT (NCT03040999, and NCT02707588). In addition, the Sponsor has conducted post-hoc exploratory analyses of EFS data from KEYNOTE-689 to assess the contribution of pembrolizumab in the neoadjuvant and adjuvant phases. These additional KEYNOTE-689 analyses will be included in Module 2.7.3. Discussion during the 11/25/2024 meeting: FDA acknowledged Merck’s response. FDA stated that the proposed information, including Merck's post-hoc exploratory analyses from KN-689, will be considered during the sBLA review. FDA stated that all of the data will be considered in the context of the KN-689 study results and the overall risk-benefit assessment for the proposed indication. FDA stated that if the ADRISK trial results were to become available during the sBLA review, Merck should submit topline results to the sBLA. PREA REQUIREMENTS Under the Pediatric Research Equity Act (PREA) (codified at section 505B of the Federal Food, Drug, and Cosmetic Act (FD&C Act), 21 U.S.C. 355c), all applications for new active ingredients (which includes new salts and new fixed combinations), new indications, new dosage forms, new dosing regimens, or new routes of administration are required to contain an assessment of the safety and effectiveness of the product for Reference ID: 5501943

<<<PAGE 378>>>
IND 122325 Page 17 U.S. Food and Drug Administration Silver Spring, MD 20993 www.fda.gov the claimed indication(s) in pediatric patients unless this requirement is waived or deferred (see section 505B(a)(1)(A) of the FD&C Act). Applications for drugs or biological products for which orphan designation has been granted that otherwise would be subject to the requirements of section 505B(a)(1)(A) are exempt pursuant to section 505B(k)(1) from the PREA requirement to conduct pediatric assessments. Title V of the FDA Reauthorization Act of 2017 (FDARA) amended the statute to create section 505B(a)(1)(B), which requires that any original marketing application for certain adult oncology drugs (i.e., those intended for treatment of an adult cancer and with molecular targets that FDA has determined to be substantially relevant to the growth or progression of a pediatric cancer) that are submitted on or after August 18, 2020, contain reports of molecularly targeted pediatric cancer investigations. See link to list of relevant molecular targets below. These molecularly targeted pediatric cancer investigations must be “designed to yield clinically meaningful pediatric study data, gathered using appropriate formulations for each age group for which the study is required, regarding dosing, safety, and preliminary efficacy to inform potential pediatric labeling” (section 505B(a)(3)). Applications for drugs or biological products for which orphan designation has been granted and which are subject to the requirements of section 505B(a)(1)(B), however, will not be exempt from PREA (see section 505B(k)(2)) and will be required to include plans to conduct the molecularly targeted pediatric investigations as required, unless such investigations are waived or deferred. Under section 505B(e)(2)(A)(i) of the FD&C Act, you must submit an Initial Pediatric Study Plan (iPSP) within 60 days of an End of Phase 2 (EOP2) meeting, or such other time as agreed upon with FDA. (In the absence of an EOP2 meeting, refer to the draft guidance below.) The iPSP must contain an outline of the pediatric assessment(s) or molecularly targeted pediatric cancer investigation(s) that you plan to conduct (including, to the extent practicable study objectives and design, age groups, relevant endpoints, and statistical approach); any request for a deferral, partial waiver, or waiver, if applicable, along with any supporting documentation; and any previously negotiated pediatric plans with other regulatory authorities. The iPSP should be submitted in PDF and Word format. Failure to include an Agreed iPSP with a marketing application could result in a refuse to file action. For additional guidance on the timing, content, and submission of the iPSP, including an iPSP Template, please refer to the guidance for industry Pediatric Study Plans: Content of and Process for Submitting Initial Pediatric Study Plans and Amended Pediatric Study Plans . For the latest version of the molecular target list, please refer to FDA.gov. 2 2 https://www.fda.gov/about-fda/oncology-center-excellence/pediatric-oncology Reference ID: 5501943

<<<PAGE 379>>>
IND 122325 Page 18 U.S. Food and Drug Administration Silver Spring, MD 20993 www.fda.gov FDARA REQUIREMENTS Sponsors planning to submit original applications on or after August 18, 2020 or sponsors who are uncertain of their submission date may request a meeting with the Oncology Center of Excellence Pediatric Oncology Program to discuss preparation of the sponsor’s initial pediatric study plan (iPSP) for a drug/biologic that is intended to treat a serious or life-threatening disease/ condition which includes addressing the amendments to PREA (Sec. 505B of the FD &C Act) for early evaluation in the pediatric population of new drugs directed at a target that the FDA deems substantively relevant to the growth or progression of one or more types of cancer in children. The purpose of these meetings will be to discuss the Agency’s current thinking about the relevance of a specific target and the specific expectations for early assessment in the pediatric population unless substantive justification for a waiver or deferral can be provided. Meetings requests should be sent to the appropriate review division with the cover letter clearly stating “ MEETING REQUEST FOR PREPARATION OF iPSP MEETING UNDER FDARA. ” These meetings will be scheduled within 30 days of meeting request receipt. The Agency strongly advises the complete meeting package be submitted at the same time as the meeting request. Sponsors should consult the guidance for industry, Formal Meetings Between the FDA and Sponsors or Applicants , to ensure open lines of dialogue before and during their drug development process. In addition, you may contact the OCE Subcommittee of PeRC Regulatory Project Manager by email at OCEPERC@fda.hhs.gov. For further guidance on pediatric product development, please refer to FDA.gov. 3 SUBMISSION FORMAT REQUIREMENTS The Electronic Common Technical Document (eCTD) is CDER and CBER’s standard format for electronic regulatory submissions. The following submission types: NDA, ANDA, BLA, Master File (except Type III) and Commercial INDs must be submitted in eCTD format. Submissions that do not adhere to the requirements stated in the eCTD Guidance will be subject to rejection. Submissions less than 10 GB must be submitted via the ESG. IND 122325 Page 19 U.S. Food and Drug Administration Silver Spring, MD 20993 www.fda.gov SECURE EMAIL COMMUNICATIONS Secure email is required for all email communications from FDA when confidential information (e.g., trade secrets, manufacturing, or patient information) is included in the message. To receive email communications from FDA that include confidential information (e.g., information requests, labeling revisions, courtesy copies of letters), you must establish secure email. Please note that secure email may not be used for formal regulatory submissions to applications (except for 7-day safety reports for INDs not in eCTD format). ONCOLOGY PILOT PROJECTS The FDA Oncology Center of Excellence (OCE) is conducting two pilot projects, the Real-Time Oncology Review (RTOR) and the Assessment Aid. RTOR is a pilot review process allowing interactive engagement with the applicant so that review and analysis of data may commence prior to full supplemental NDA/BLA submission. Assessment Aid is a voluntary submission from the applicant to facilitate FDA’s assessment of the NDA/BLA application (original or supplemental). An applicant can communicate interest in participating in these pilot programs to the FDA review division by sending a notification to the Regulatory Project Manager when the top-line results of a pivotal trial are available or at the pre-sNDA/sBLA meeting. Those applicants who do not wish to participate in the pilot programs will follow the usual submission process with no impact on review timelines or benefit-risk decisions. More information on these pilot programs, including eligibility criteria and timelines, can be found at the following FDA websites: x RTOR 6 : In general, the data submission should be fully CDISC-compliant to facilitate efficient review. x Assessment Aid 7 SPLIT REAL TIME APPLICATION REVIEW (STAR) PILOT PROGRAM The FDA is conducting a pilot program, the Split Real Time Application Review (STAR). STAR is a pilot review process allowing earlier patient access to therapies that address an unmet medical need via interactive engagement with the application so that review and analysis of data may commence prior to full supplemental new drug application (sNDA)/supplemental biologics license application (sBLA) submission. An applicant can communicate interest in participating in this pilot program to the FDA review division by submitting a STAR Entry Request if the applicant believes a proposed efficacy supplement fulfills the conditions outlined in the eligibility criteria. Those applicants who do not wish to participate in the pilot program will follow the usual submission process 6 https://www.fda.gov/about-fda/oncology-center-excellence/real-time-oncology-review- pilot-program 7 https://www.fda.gov/about-fda/oncology-center-excellence/assessment-aid-pilot-project Reference ID: 5501943
